───── ASSISTANT · 2026-08-27 00:04:28 ─────
Very good catch — both patterns are exactly the kind of thing that causes tools to either skip work silently or overwrite good data. Your two scenarios map to these rules, which I'm building in:

1. **`sub`/`subs`/`Subtitles` folders** adjacent to (or below) the video get scanned: any *detectably-English* file there marks the video covered; only foreign-language sets (e.g. just `Arabic.srt`) still trigger a download.
2. **Fake/stub `<name>.srt` sidecars** (typically 1–10 KB — often actually saved HTML error pages) stop counting as "covered": sidecars below a size threshold get content-verified (real cue timings + language script check) before being trusted. The real subs in the `subs` folder are then preferred. Downloads always land as `<name>.en.srt`, so genuine files are never overwritten.

# Done — your subtitle pipeline is live 🎬

**Installed at `C:\Users\Munna\SubtitleFetcher`** — one zero-dependency Node script + README + launcher. The daily task is armed: **first automatic run today at 13:05**, then every day.

## Proven working on your real library
| Proof | Result |
|---|---|
| Batch test (6 titles) | **4 downloaded in ~6s** — even obscure Bollywood rips matched to their exact releases |
| Your fake-stub case | `<name>.srt` HTML garbage no longer counts as covered — content-verified first |
| Your `Subs` folder case | ~648 movies auto-credited from bundled `English.srt` packs with zero downloads |
| Library census | 18,282 videos → **14,044 queued** and shrinking daily |

## How it runs
```
node subtitles-fetcher.mjs status    progress dashboard
node subtitles-fetcher.mjs dry       peek at next N in queue
node subtitles-fetcher.mjs retry X   force-retry paths containing X
node subtitles-fetcher.mjs probe "…\movie.mkv"   debug one file
schedule 21:30 / --remove            manage the daily job
```
Each run stops exactly when SubDL's ~300/day cap trips (queue survives; resume tomorrow). At that pace: full coverage in **~6–7 weeks**, hands-free. Failed lookups park themselves after 3 tries so they don't clog the queue.

## One optional upgrade left
Your OpenSubtitles free API key (`setup`) isn't in yet — until then the app runs SubDL-only. Adding it gives a second brain for the stubborn leftovers (the 2 misses in my batch are exactly its territory) plus hash-perfect sync confirmation.

One thing to know: multiple `.en.srt` files may appear in your Jellyfin as "English (full)" tracks alongside existing embedded subs — normal sidecar behavior. Enjoy!

## 1. Per-run log files → `logs\`
- **`run-YYYY-MM-DD.log`** — every invocation appends here (ANSI colors stripped, each line ISO-timestamped):
```
07:41:02 queue: 6 (limited run) via [sd]
07:41:03  ✔ movies/eagle (2024) …  « EAGLE (2024) 720p AMZN WEB-DL… »   ← success + exact release used

 ✖ …elvis.and.anabelle….mkv — no provider had it (attempt 2)
```
- **`scan-YYYY-MM-DD.log`** — inventory scan results.
- **`task.log`** — scheduled-task runs capture *everything* the daily job printed (it's the redirected stdout), so mornings you can just open it and see overnight results.

## 2. The machine-readable ledger → `state.json`
Every video in the library has a record that accumulates history across days:
```json
"aashiq banaya aapne….mkv": {
  "status": "done",          // covered | pending | failed | parked | done
  "provider": "sd",
  "rel": "Aashiq Banaya Aapne 2005 WebRip Hindi 720p ESub - mkvCinem",
  "when": "2026-08-27T07:42:11Z",
  "attempts": 1
}
```
Failed ones carry `lastError`, `attempts`, and `when` — which is how the retry/park logic knows when to stop trying a hopeless title.

Quick peek without reading raw JSON:
```bat
node subtitles-fetcher.mjs status        :: totals
node subtitles-fetcher.mjs dry 15        :: next 15 in the queue
```

One nuance to know: the ✖ example above is real — "FIFA World Cup 2026 final" isn't going to be on any subtitle site until it happens 🙂; after 3 strikes such items auto-*park* (out of the active queue, out of your nightly logs' way), retrievable anytime with `retry worldcup`.

## New-user flow (exactly what you asked)
```bash
git clone … && cd subtitle-fetcher
node subtitles-fetcher.mjs setup        # or run install.cmd / ./install.sh
```
The wizard walks through:
1. **Library folders** (any number; UNC or POSIX paths accepted)
2. **Optional OpenSubtitles key** (SubDL works anonymously from minute one)
3. **Daily schedule time** → registers automatically for their OS:
   - Windows: Task Scheduler entry `SubtitleFetcherDaily` + a generated `run-daily.cmd` launcher
   - Linux/macOS: a *marked* line appended to crontab (`# subtitle-fetcher`) using the absolute node path, so nvm setups work; removed cleanly by `schedule --remove`

| candidate | verdict |

## What the new provider does
- **TV-only by design**: searches return nothing for movies → they fall through to SubDL untouched. Chain is now `a7 → sd → os` *per item*.
- **Handles both search modes**: unique matches get a **302 straight to the episode page** (WandaVision); multi-match shows get a results list (Breaking Bad). Both parsed.
- **Release-aware like everything else**: you got proper team versions per episode — the WandaVision run picked `1080p.CM-CMRG+ION10+KOGi+TOMMY`, `EVO+TOMMY+ION10`, etc. Hearing-impaired flag detected from version headers.
- **Ban-safe pacing**: one global ~3.2 s gap between all Addic7ed hits plus automatic cooldown if it ever says "too many requests" — after which remaining episodes just fall through to SubDL for the night.

# Plan delivered — `docs/plan-web-service.md` (pushed, `73eb5ea` + amendment)

## Key decisions in the plan

**Evolve, don't rebuild.** The irreplaceable value in this codebase isn't structure — it's the empirical scar tissue: SubDL's referer guard, Addic7ed's 302-vs-list duality, the underscore parser trap, fake-stub detection. A rewrite re-pays that tuition. So: Phase 0 extracts the proven core into `lib/` modules byte-identically, then the service grows around it. You get the new architecture without new risk.

**Your requirements, mapped:**
| You asked | Plan section |
|---|---|
| One `git clone` + single install command | `install.mjs` — interactive or flags, writes systemd unit (Linux) / boot scheduled task (Windows), health-checks, prints the LAN URL with token |
| Runs detached at startup like Jellyfin/qBittorrent | §7 — systemd `enable --now`; Windows `schtasks /SC ONSTART` (zero extra tools, NSSM documented as alternative) |
| LAN web UI, own port | default **:8097** (next to your 8090/8096), dark vanilla-JS dashboard, no build step |
| Pick movie & TV folders in UI | Folders tab with **server-side directory browser** (browsers can't browse the server's disks — the API can) |
| Status / reports / current activities | Dashboard tiles + **SSE live activity feed**; `/report` per-day charts; `activity.jsonl` append-only event log |
| Browse what it downloaded | Library tab: shows → seasons → episodes grid, movie grid |
| **Re-search & swap a subtitle** | The deepest API change: `/items/:key/candidates` lists ranked alternatives *without downloading*, one click swaps (old file kept as `.en.srt.1`, history records the swap) |
| Detached process | single long-running `service.mjs`; the listening port doubles as the instance lock; CLI becomes a remote control when the service is up |

**Security default:** LAN-bind + auto-generated token (installer prints the bookmark-ready URL) — usable from any device, useless to a nosy neighbor.

**Phases:** 0 extraction → 1 service+installer+dashboard → 2 folders/queue/settings → 3 library+swap → 4 reports. Each lands working software.

## Meanwhile, your desktop run today: mission accomplished then cap-hit

- **272 subtitles downloaded** today until SubDL's ~300/day allowance ran out
- Bonus discovery: the cap enforces as a **silent soft block** — pages come back 200-OK with results *stripped*, so misses masquerade as "no subtitles exist". I've already encoded the detection rule for Phase 1 into the plan (streak-detection + retroactive attempt-counter forgiveness)
- The run is still alive and now churning through remaining movies (harmless misses, auto-retry tomorrow) — once it reaches TV items, **Addic7ed kicks in unaffected** and keeps delivering tonight
- Tomorrow 13:05: fresh SubDL quota + the queue continues exactly where it stopped

When you're ready to build it, say the word and Phase 0 + 1 are the natural first swing — that alone gets you the service, installer, and dashboard on the M93p.

## What you got in this swing

**Engine intelligence (the important one):** the SubDL silent-cap behavior we discovered yesterday is now *handled in code* — after ~40 consecutive empty searches on a day with 100+ downloads, sd retires itself for the UTC day and every attempt it wasted during the streak is retroactively forgiven (re-queued, attempts refunded). Your old queue items that "failed" during yesterday's soft block will be re-attempted normally.

**Dashboard:** live activity feed (SSE), stat tiles, provider pills with quota, last-run card, **LAN URL card** (auto-detects the machine's LAN IP), and a **14-day downloads bar chart** with provider-share breakdown.

**Folders:** per-root reachability dots + covered/queued counts per folder.

**Item modal (queue + library):** status, release, errors, **history timeline**, park/un-park, re-queue, and the full **"Find alternatives" → swap** flow (providers searched live, one click downloads & swaps, old subtitle kept as `.srt.1`).

**Settings:** behavior toggles, provider order, schedule, OpenSubtitles credentials, and **access-token regeneration** (old token stays valid until restart so you can't lock yourself out).

Everything live-smoked before push: status with per-root stats + LAN URL (`http://192.168.0.100:8097`), park endpoint, 14/14 tests green.

## Deploy day checklist (when you're ready)
1. On the M93p: install Node ≥ 20, then `git clone` + `node install.mjs` — bookmark the printed LAN URL
2. Point it at the server's *local* folders via the Folders tab, hit **Scan**
3. Tell me — I'll disable the desktop's scheduled task and today's leftover queue state won't matter (a fresh local scan re-derives everything from the `.en.srt` files already on disk, by design)

The desktop's CLI (`cli.mjs`) keeps working as a remote control/read-only viewer in the meantime. Enjoy the dashboard 🙂

───── ASSISTANT · 2026-08-28 15:40:35 ─────
Fixed, pushed (`git log: fix: empty modal was stuck open`). **Do one hard refresh — Ctrl+F5** — and the whole dashboard appears underneath; it was there all along.

**Root cause:** CSS specificity footgun. The stylesheet set `#modal { display:flex }`, which outranks the browser's built-in `[hidden] { display:none }` rule. So the hidden attribute on the overlay was ignored — an empty modal with a lone Close button covered the page from the moment it loaded, and clicking Close *did* set `hidden=true`… which the CSS kept overriding. The UI underneath never had a chance.

**What changed:**
- `#modal[hidden] { display:none }` — the toggle now actually works
- Clicking the dark backdrop or pressing **Escape** also closes it
- Static files now serve with `Cache-Control: no-cache`, so future fixes reach your browser on a normal refresh instead of needing cache-busting

No service restart was needed — static files are read from disk per request, so the corrected CSS/JS are already being served. Your fetch engine kept working through all of this (it's server-side); the queue has been moving the whole time.

───── ASSISTANT · 2026-08-28 15:45:38 ─────
Both done and live. **Refresh the page (F5)** for the feed fix; the service is already running the new engine.

## 1. Feed scroll — fixed
The feed now appends lines instead of rebuilding, and auto-scrolls **only while you're already at the bottom** (48px threshold). Scroll up and it stays put; scroll back to the bottom and it pins again. It also backfills the last 60 events on page load so you land on recent history.

## 2. The logic — your understanding is ~90% right

```
per video:  try a7 → try sd → try os (inactive until you add a free key)
   movie :  a7 skips it by design  →  SubDL does the work
episode :  Addic7ed first (release-team matched, community-synced)
           if a7 lacks it → SubDL → (OS later)
```

- **SubDL**: ~**300 downloads/day per IP**, shared by everything that reaches it. Plus the nastiness we discovered: when exhausted it doesn't error — it serves empty results silently. The engine now detects that streak, retires sd for the rest of the UTC day, and forgives the attempts it wasted.
- **Addic7ed**: no numeric daily cap — **but "no limit" ≠ "no rules."** It throttles hard when hammered, so we pace every a7 request ~3.2 s apart and cool down when it complains. Practically that's a ceiling of ~1,000/hour, and we deliberately stay human-paced. So yes — **TV effectively runs 24/7**, as it did last night (~940 subs in an evening).

**The flaw you almost caught:** queue order runs movies (a→z) before TV — and once SubDL's day ended, every remaining *movie* still got tried daily, failed instantly (a7 skips movies), burned its 3 attempts, and **parked permanently**. That's the opposite of what you'd want. Fixed just now:

> **New rule:** if no source can serve an item today, it's **deferred — zero attempts spent**. So tonight the engine zoomed past the remaining movies (no penalty) and went straight to living on Addic7ed TV episodes all night. Movies resume automatically when SubDL's quota resets.

Restarted the service to activate all this — verified: engine `fetching`, currently on a Bengali series (`aaj robibar S01E02`), with sd-capped movies being cleanly deferred behind it.

## What you got

**🎬 Library, the media-center way**
- **TV Shows / Movies** as proper segmented tabs, with a **live search box** (filters shows and titles as you type)
- **Poster grid** — movie cards show real art pulled from your Jellyfin folders (`poster.jpg`/`folder.jpg`/`backdrop.jpg`/`logo.png`, verified serving The Firm's actual 56KB poster + 77KB backdrop); shows get thumbnails in the accordion header
- No art on disk and no OMDB key? Elegant gradient placeholder with the title's initials — never a broken image
- **OMDB fallback**: paste a free key (omdbapi.com) into *Settings → Poster art fallback* and titles without local art automatically get their official poster (cached in `cache/omdb.json`, lazy-loaded so it never floods the API)
- Click any card/episode → the detail modal with **"Find alternatives…"** — ranked candidates from all providers, one click swaps, previous subtitle kept as `.srt.1`. Works exactly the same whether or not a subtitle already exists on disk
- Episode chips show status at a glance (✓ green / ✗ dim), hover shows which release was downloaded

**⏩ Priority fetch** — "Fetch now (priority)" on any item, or "fetch missing" on a whole show. If a fetch is running, your item is spliced into the front of the live queue within seconds; if a scan is running, a scoped side-runner grabs it immediately (Addic7ed's pacer keeps both polite); if idle, it runs instantly.

**Titles fixed** — proper case restored from the original paths (`#Stuck`, not `#stuck`), padded folder numbering stripped (`001 Puss Gets The Boot` → *Puss Gets The Boot*) while genuine numeric titles (`1`, `10`, `21`) survive, and junk folders (`720p`) no longer masquerade as show names.

**Extras from your messages:** Logs page now lists files newest-first with sizes and explains empty files instead of showing nothing; icons are Feather's SVGs inlined directly (same look as React-feather, zero build step); and on **SQLite** — you're right it's the right eventual store (`node:sqlite`, built into Node 22+, still zero-dep). At 18k items JSON is holding fine (~5MB, written every 5 items), so it's queued as the upgrade when the library or query load outgrows it — the store layer is already isolated for that swap.

───── ASSISTANT · 2026-08-28 18:36:55 ─────
All fixed and live (`d2326a1`) — refresh the Library page.

## What was wrong

1. **Adventure Time's poster**: the art resolver only checked the *episode's own folder* for TV — Adventure Time keeps its `folder.jpg` in the show folder above. Now TV art walks **episode dir → show dir → grandparent**, and poster requests may fall back to `backdrop.jpg`/`fanart.jpg` when no portrait art exists (your "show either" rule). Verified: Adventure Time now serves its 247KB `folder.jpg`. *(The "Adenture" spelling is in the actual folder name on the NAS — Jellyfin matches it fine either way.)*

2. **OMDB posters now persist into your library**: when local art is missing and an OMDB key is configured, the fetched poster is downloaded and saved **into the show/movie folder as `poster.jpg`** (backdrops as `backdrop.jpg`) — so Jellyfin picks it up too, not just this dashboard. Magic-byte validated, never overwrites existing files, and the fetch failure path still falls back to hot-linking so the UI always shows something. Add your free key in *Settings → Poster art fallback* to activate.

3. **Lowercase titles, root-caused**: items restored from the v1 era had no cached metadata, and the scanner only backfilled metadata when a file's mtime changed — so most items were permanently falling back to their lowercase path key. The scanner now **always backfills** metadata (a rescan is running in the background to fill it all in), and the display layer additionally prettifies show names, so everything reads "Adventure Time" regardless.

4. **Tab switching hardened**: bindings restructured with error toasts — if anything breaks client-side again, you'll see a red toast with the actual message instead of silent dead buttons.

Once the background scan completes (watch the phase indicator), poster art and titles will be consistent across all 18k items.

───── ASSISTANT · 2026-08-28 19:36:32 ─────
Both mysteries solved, fixed, and verified live — refresh the Library page:

## Why TV years "never appeared"
The scan you were waiting on **wasn't done** — it's genuinely that slow over WiFi/SMB (the async walk still does a per-file stat + subtitle-sniff over the network; ~40+ minutes for 18k files, and the previous two attempts were killed by my service restarts mid-flight). No completion → no persisted metadata → no years.

So I made labels **not depend on the scan at all**: the library view now derives show name + years directly from the original-cased folder path in the state keys. Verified immediately:

```
Drake And Josh (2004 - 2008) · Aaj Robibar (1996 - 1999) · Adventure Time (2010)
Ben 10 (2005) · Chernobyl (2019)      — 21 shows with year ranges, live now
```

## Why Drake & Josh was "$1"
A genuine parser bug, and a fun one: the show-name cleaner stripped `- Complete Series` suffixes with a `replace(regex, "$1")` — but the regex had **no capture groups**, and in JavaScript an unmatched `$1` in a replacement string is inserted **literally**. Every "… - Complete …" folder became a show named *"$1"*. Fixed with a proper prefix-capturing pattern. (A fresh scan is now running in the background with the fixed parser, so cached metadata will be clean too — labels already look right regardless.)

Both fixes pushed. Worth noting for later: once this moves to the M93p with local-disk access, scans drop from ~40 minutes to a couple — that's when the "Scan" button becomes genuinely instant-feeling.
